========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... my wife made the appointment. She's the one who's scared. We've been in that house for fifteen years, and honestly, the maintenance is just... it's a lot. I haven't been working, so I try to keep an eye on things, but the plumbing is old. We had a guy out a few years ago, charged us an arm and a leg just to look at the water heater. He drove this loud yellow van that leaked oil all over my driveway, which took me two days to scrub out. 

But anyway, what finally scared her—why she made me come in—is that I keep hearing water running inside the walls. And men whispering. Just... whispering in the walls, when nobody’s there. I thought maybe it was the neighbors at first, but our houses aren't even that close. And sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I go over, I touch it, I check it... and it's dry. Completely dry. I used to lay down towels, you know, the good blue ones we got for our anniversary, just in case the wood warped, but... I don't know. My brother had water damage once and it completely ruined his foundation, so you can't be too careful.

Well, let's see... it's been going on for about seven months now. Yeah, right around the time of that awful winter freeze we had. You remember how bad the roads got? I actually had to go out in it because my wife's sedan has terrible traction. Those tires were practically bald since we bought them at that discount place off the interstate. I told her not to go there, the guys in the shop always try to upsell you on alignment packages you don't even need, but she likes a bargain. 

Anyway, the freeze... it just turned all the house repairs I was trying to manage into this massive mess. I just couldn't get out from under it. I had been trying to patch up the baseboards in the guest room, and then the cold hit, and the materials wouldn't set right, and I was spending hours just sitting on the floor staring at the putty... I don't know. It just felt like everything was piling up and collapsing all at once. But yeah, that's when I started hearing them. Seven months. Ever since the freeze ruined everything.

People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. 

The wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. 

And the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.

It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. 

But... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.

Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year.

I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.

skip meals without noticing and my jeans have gotten loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: My wife says I move like I’m underwater now, and even answering a simple question about a repair estimate can take me so long that people start talking for me.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Just walking from the kitchen to the back room to look at the tools can wear me out, and resting on the couch doesn’t bring the energy back.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before the house problems dragged on and I fell into this depression, I never heard voices in the walls, saw wet patches that weren’t there, or thought the neighbors were tracking me. Those things only showed up after I had already stopped caring about my usual life and started feeling like a failure every day.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’ve stopped keeping up with basic repairs, bills, and commitments, and my wife has had to handle decisions I used to manage myself. Things got bad enough that she called a crisis team because I couldn’t cooperate with a safety inspection at the house.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since the winter freeze turned the house repairs into a mess I couldn’t get out from under.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think the whispering in the walls is actually people talking, or could it be something else?\n\nPatient: People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. \n\nThe wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. \n\nAnd the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.\n\nDoctor: Do you really think Gary is watching you, or is that just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. And they know about the house. I'm telling you, the mold and that old wiring, it has completely poisoned everything I own. Everything in there is contaminated. My clothes, my tools... \n\nI had this really good set of Craftsman wrenches I bought at Sears, back when the one at the mall was still open. You remember that mall? They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. I don't know who would want to sit on a glass balcony right over the interstate, the noise must be terrible. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can do anything to stop Gary from watching you or to prevent the house from getting worse?\n\nPatient: Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. Actually, it was the exact same day I bought this heavy-duty bleach to try and scrub the walls. People think bleach kills mold, but it really doesn't. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year. \n\nBut... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, the house. Right. Like I said before, I know it sounds far-fetched. I know exactly how it sounds to a doctor. But I am just... I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you have any control over what happens to you and your house?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Control? No, no control at all. I mean, how can I? Everything is already ruined. I haven't been able to keep up with any of it... the repairs, paying the bills, none of it. My wife, she’s had to take over everything. All the decisions I used to handle, she’s doing it now. She's been so stressed, I can see it in her face. She used to love gardening to relax, you know? She had these beautiful tomato plants out back, these huge Big Boys and some cherry tomatoes. We used to make our own salsa every August. We'd get the jalapeños from the farmer's market downtown... the one they hold on Saturdays next to the old courthouse. They have this guy there who sells the most incredible raw honey. He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... 

But, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.

Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again.

Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... 

Wait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.

Harm me? Like... physically? No, I don't think they're going to come over here and, you know, attack me or anything. It's not like that. It's more like they're just waiting for me to fail. Like I told you, I know how crazy it sounds, I really do. But I'm telling you, I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Everything is ruined. And I can just tell the neighbors are watching, waiting to see when I mess up again. 

It's like they have nothing better to do with their time. Especially since the neighborhood association stopped doing those monthly block parties. They used to have them every first Sunday of the month, right down at the end of the cul-de-sac. Someone would always bring this massive inflatable bounce house for the kids. One time, it got really windy, and the stakes came loose, and the whole thing almost blew into Mrs. Gable's prized rose bushes. She would have been furious. She spends hours out there with these tiny little pruning shears, clipping away at them. She won first prize at the county fair three years in a row for her yellow roses. My wife tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles just decimated them. We tried spraying them with this organic neem oil stuff, but it smelled terrible, like rancid garlic and peanut butter...

But, yeah, no, they don't want to hurt me physically. They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.

We had a guy out a few years ago, charged us an arm and a leg just to look at the water heater. He drove this loud yellow van that leaked oil all over my driveway, which took me two days to scrub out. \n\nBut anyway, what finally scared her—why she made me come in—is that I keep hearing water running inside the walls. And men whispering. Just... whispering in the walls, when nobody’s there. I thought maybe it was the neighbors at first, but our houses aren't even that close. And sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I go over, I touch it, I check it... and it's dry. Completely dry. I used to lay down towels, you know, the good blue ones we got for our anniversary, just in case the wood warped, but... I don't know. My brother had water damage once and it completely ruined his foundation, so you can't be too careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been going on for about seven months now. Yeah, right around the time of that awful winter freeze we had. You remember how bad the roads got? I actually had to go out in it because my wife's sedan has terrible traction. Those tires were practically bald since we bought them at that discount place off the interstate. I told her not to go there, the guys in the shop always try to upsell you on alignment packages you don't even need, but she likes a bargain. \n\nAnyway, the freeze... it just turned all the house repairs I was trying to manage into this massive mess. I just couldn't get out from under it. I had been trying to patch up the baseboards in the guest room, and then the cold hit, and the materials wouldn't set right, and I was spending hours just sitting on the floor staring at the putty... I don't know. It just felt like everything was piling up and collapsing all at once. But yeah, that's when I started hearing them. Seven months. Ever since the freeze ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the whispering in the walls is actually people talking, or could it be something else?\n\nPatient: People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. \n\nThe wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. \n\nAnd the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.\n\nDoctor: Do you really think Gary is watching you, or is that just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. And they know about the house. I'm telling you, the mold and that old wiring, it has completely poisoned everything I own. Everything in there is contaminated. My clothes, my tools... \n\nI had this really good set of Craftsman wrenches I bought at Sears, back when the one at the mall was still open. You remember that mall? They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. I don't know who would want to sit on a glass balcony right over the interstate, the noise must be terrible. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can do anything to stop Gary from watching you or to prevent the house from getting worse?\n\nPatient: Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. Actually, it was the exact same day I bought this heavy-duty bleach to try and scrub the walls. People think bleach kills mold, but it really doesn't. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year. \n\nBut... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, the house. Right. Like I said before, I know it sounds far-fetched. I know exactly how it sounds to a doctor. But I am just... I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have any control over what happens to you and your house?\n\nPatient: Control? No, no control at all. I mean, how can I? Everything is already ruined. I haven't been able to keep up with any of it... the repairs, paying the bills, none of it. My wife, she’s had to take over everything. All the decisions I used to handle, she’s doing it now. She's been so stressed, I can see it in her face. She used to love gardening to relax, you know? She had these beautiful tomato plants out back, these huge Big Boys and some cherry tomatoes. We used to make our own salsa every August. We'd get the jalapeños from the farmer's market downtown... the one they hold on Saturdays next to the old courthouse. They have this guy there who sells the most incredible raw honey. He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... \n\nBut, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nGary especially. He'll stand out by his mailbox forever, just staring at my place. He gets his mail from that same guy we've had on our route for fifteen years. Carl, I think his name is. Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... \n\nWait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Like... physically? No, I don't think they're going to come over here and, you know, attack me or anything. It's not like that. It's more like they're just waiting for me to fail. Like I told you, I know how crazy it sounds, I really do. But I'm telling you, I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Everything is ruined. And I can just tell the neighbors are watching, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nIt's like they have nothing better to do with their time. Especially since the neighborhood association stopped doing those monthly block parties. They used to have them every first Sunday of the month, right down at the end of the cul-de-sac. Someone would always bring this massive inflatable bounce house for the kids. One time, it got really windy, and the stakes came loose, and the whole thing almost blew into Mrs. Gable's prized rose bushes. She would have been furious. She spends hours out there with these tiny little pruning shears, clipping away at them. She won first prize at the county fair three years in a row for her yellow roses. My wife tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles just decimated them. We tried spraying them with this organic neem oil stuff, but it smelled terrible, like rancid garlic and peanut butter...\n\nBut, yeah, no, they don't want to hurt me physically. They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Talking about me? Yeah... I mean, I see them out there, leaning over the property lines. They whisper. It’s like high school all over again. My daughter hated high school for that exact reason. She went to West Monroe, and the girls there were just brutal. She had this one friend, Sarah, who spread a rumor about her stealing a graphing calculator from the math lab. Can you believe that? A TI-84. Those things were like a hundred bucks back then. I remember buying it at Circuit City before they all closed down. Circuit City had that big red plug logo... I used to love wandering the aisles there on weekends, looking at the huge stereo systems. We bought our first CD player there in the nineties, it was a Sony, held five discs at once... 

But, um, what were we... right, the neighbors. Yes, they talk about me. I know I keep saying this today, and I know you probably think it's completely far-fetched, but they are definitely watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. They know about the house. They know that the mold and the bad wiring have completely poisoned everything I own. They're just standing out there whispering about it, waiting for me to finally lose it.

He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... \n\nBut, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nGary especially. He'll stand out by his mailbox forever, just staring at my place. He gets his mail from that same guy we've had on our route for fifteen years. Carl, I think his name is. Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... \n\nWait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Like... physically? No, I don't think they're going to come over here and, you know, attack me or anything. It's not like that. It's more like they're just waiting for me to fail. Like I told you, I know how crazy it sounds, I really do. But I'm telling you, I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Everything is ruined. And I can just tell the neighbors are watching, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nIt's like they have nothing better to do with their time. One time, it got really windy, and the stakes came loose, and the whole thing almost blew into Mrs. Gable's prized rose bushes. She would have been furious. My wife tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles just decimated them. We tried spraying them with this organic neem oil stuff, but it smelled terrible, like rancid garlic and peanut butter...\n\nBut, yeah, no, they don't want to hurt me physically. They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are talking about you specifically when they're outside?\n\nPatient: Talking about me? Yeah... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, when they whisper... I mean, what else would they be whispering about? It's always when I'm out there or when they think I'm looking out the window. They huddle up. Just like those guys at the hardware store used to do. I worked at a True Value for about six months back in my twenties. The manager, this guy named Phil, he was always whispering with the cashiers. Phil drove this beat-up old El Camino, faded blue with a rusted-out tailgate. He was so proud of that car, always talking about how he was going to restore it, put a new engine in it, paint it cherry red. He kept a bucket of those pine tree air fresheners in the cab, so it always smelled like cheap cologne and cedar... 

Anyway, I don't even know why I thought of Phil. But yes, the neighbors. They're whispering about me. Like I keep telling you—and I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do—but I am certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It's all ruined. And they know it. They're just watching and whispering, waiting to see when I mess up again.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... my wife made the appointment. She's the one who's scared. We've been in that house for fifteen years, and honestly, the maintenance is just... it's a lot. I haven't been working, so I try to keep an eye on things, but the plumbing is old. We had a guy out a few years ago, charged us an arm and a leg just to look at the water heater. He drove this loud yellow van that leaked oil all over my driveway, which took me two days to scrub out. \n\nBut anyway, what finally scared her—why she made me come in—is that I keep hearing water running inside the walls. And men whispering. Just... whispering in the walls, when nobody’s there. I thought maybe it was the neighbors at first, but our houses aren't even that close. And sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I go over, I touch it, I check it... and it's dry. Completely dry. I used to lay down towels, you know, the good blue ones we got for our anniversary, just in case the wood warped, but... I don't know. My brother had water damage once and it completely ruined his foundation, so you can't be too careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been going on for about seven months now. Yeah, right around the time of that awful winter freeze we had. You remember how bad the roads got? I actually had to go out in it because my wife's sedan has terrible traction. Those tires were practically bald since we bought them at that discount place off the interstate. I told her not to go there, the guys in the shop always try to upsell you on alignment packages you don't even need, but she likes a bargain. \n\nAnyway, the freeze... it just turned all the house repairs I was trying to manage into this massive mess. I just couldn't get out from under it. I had been trying to patch up the baseboards in the guest room, and then the cold hit, and the materials wouldn't set right, and I was spending hours just sitting on the floor staring at the putty... I don't know. It just felt like everything was piling up and collapsing all at once. But yeah, that's when I started hearing them. Seven months. Ever since the freeze ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the whispering in the walls is actually people talking, or could it be something else?\n\nPatient: People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. \n\nThe wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. \n\nAnd the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.\n\nDoctor: Do you really think Gary is watching you, or is that just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. And they know about the house. I'm telling you, the mold and that old wiring, it has completely poisoned everything I own. Everything in there is contaminated. My clothes, my tools... \n\nI had this really good set of Craftsman wrenches I bought at Sears, back when the one at the mall was still open. You remember that mall? They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. I don't know who would want to sit on a glass balcony right over the interstate, the noise must be terrible. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can do anything to stop Gary from watching you or to prevent the house from getting worse?\n\nPatient: Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. Actually, it was the exact same day I bought this heavy-duty bleach to try and scrub the walls. People think bleach kills mold, but it really doesn't. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year. \n\nBut... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, the house. Right. Like I said before, I know it sounds far-fetched. I know exactly how it sounds to a doctor. But I am just... I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have any control over what happens to you and your house?\n\nPatient: Control? No, no control at all. I mean, how can I? Everything is already ruined. I haven't been able to keep up with any of it... the repairs, paying the bills, none of it. My wife, she’s had to take over everything. All the decisions I used to handle, she’s doing it now. She's been so stressed, I can see it in her face. She used to love gardening to relax, you know? She had these beautiful tomato plants out back, these huge Big Boys and some cherry tomatoes. We used to make our own salsa every August. We'd get the jalapeños from the farmer's market downtown... the one they hold on Saturdays next to the old courthouse. They have this guy there who sells the most incredible raw honey. He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... \n\nBut, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are talking about you specifically when they're outside?\n\nPatient: Talking about me? Yeah... I mean, I see them out there, leaning over the property lines. They whisper. It’s like high school all over again. My daughter hated high school for that exact reason. She went to West Monroe, and the girls there were just brutal. She had this one friend, Sarah, who spread a rumor about her stealing a graphing calculator from the math lab. Can you believe that? A TI-84. Those things were like a hundred bucks back then. I remember buying it at Circuit City before they all closed down. Circuit City had that big red plug logo... I used to love wandering the aisles there on weekends, looking at the huge stereo systems. We bought our first CD player there in the nineties, it was a Sony, held five discs at once... \n\nBut, um, what were we... right, the neighbors. Yes, they talk about me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it's about me. I mean, they're not whispering about the weather, you know? They look right at my house. It's like when you go to a restaurant and you know the waiter is talking about you to the bartender. We used to go to this diner, Mel's, over on 5th Street. They had the best cherry pie. Not that canned stuff, but real cherries, tart and sweet. My wife loved it. We'd go every Friday night, sit in the same red vinyl booth... booth number four, right by the jukebox. That jukebox had all the classics. I remember playing 'Dock of the Bay' on it once, and the needle got stuck, just kept repeating the same whistled note over and over until the cook had to come out and smack the side of the machine. He had this big grease stain on his apron...

But, uh, anyway, I know I've said this a hundred times today, and I know how crazy it sounds. I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's all ruined. And the neighbors, they know it. They're just watching, whispering, waiting for me to mess up again.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... my wife made the appointment. She's the one who's scared. We've been in that house for fifteen years, and honestly, the maintenance is just... it's a lot. I haven't been working, so I try to keep an eye on things, but the plumbing is old. We had a guy out a few years ago, charged us an arm and a leg just to look at the water heater. He drove this loud yellow van that leaked oil all over my driveway, which took me two days to scrub out. \n\nBut anyway, what finally scared her—why she made me come in—is that I keep hearing water running inside the walls. And men whispering. Just... whispering in the walls, when nobody’s there. I thought maybe it was the neighbors at first, but our houses aren't even that close. And sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I go over, I touch it, I check it... and it's dry. Completely dry. I used to lay down towels, you know, the good blue ones we got for our anniversary, just in case the wood warped, but... I don't know. My brother had water damage once and it completely ruined his foundation, so you can't be too careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been going on for about seven months now. Yeah, right around the time of that awful winter freeze we had. You remember how bad the roads got? I actually had to go out in it because my wife's sedan has terrible traction. Those tires were practically bald since we bought them at that discount place off the interstate. I told her not to go there, the guys in the shop always try to upsell you on alignment packages you don't even need, but she likes a bargain. \n\nAnyway, the freeze... it just turned all the house repairs I was trying to manage into this massive mess. I just couldn't get out from under it. I had been trying to patch up the baseboards in the guest room, and then the cold hit, and the materials wouldn't set right, and I was spending hours just sitting on the floor staring at the putty... I don't know. It just felt like everything was piling up and collapsing all at once. But yeah, that's when I started hearing them. Seven months. Ever since the freeze ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the whispering in the walls is actually people talking, or could it be something else?\n\nPatient: People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. \n\nThe wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. \n\nAnd the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.\n\nDoctor: Do you really think Gary is watching you, or is that just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. And they know about the house. I'm telling you, the mold and that old wiring, it has completely poisoned everything I own. Everything in there is contaminated. My clothes, my tools... \n\nI had this really good set of Craftsman wrenches I bought at Sears, back when the one at the mall was still open. You remember that mall? They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. I don't know who would want to sit on a glass balcony right over the interstate, the noise must be terrible. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can do anything to stop Gary from watching you or to prevent the house from getting worse?\n\nPatient: Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. Actually, it was the exact same day I bought this heavy-duty bleach to try and scrub the walls. People think bleach kills mold, but it really doesn't. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year. \n\nBut... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, the house. Right. Like I said before, I know it sounds far-fetched. I know exactly how it sounds to a doctor. But I am just... I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have any control over what happens to you and your house?\n\nPatient: Control? No, no control at all. I mean, how can I? Everything is already ruined. I haven't been able to keep up with any of it... the repairs, paying the bills, none of it. My wife, she’s had to take over everything. All the decisions I used to handle, she’s doing it now. She's been so stressed, I can see it in her face. She used to love gardening to relax, you know? She had these beautiful tomato plants out back, these huge Big Boys and some cherry tomatoes. We used to make our own salsa every August. We'd get the jalapeños from the farmer's market downtown... the one they hold on Saturdays next to the old courthouse. They have this guy there who sells the most incredible raw honey. He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... \n\nBut, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nGary especially. He'll stand out by his mailbox forever, just staring at my place. He gets his mail from that same guy we've had on our route for fifteen years. Carl, I think his name is. Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... \n\nWait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Like... physically? Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nGary especially. He'll stand out by his mailbox forever, just staring at my place. He gets his mail from that same guy we've had on our route for fifteen years. Carl, I think his name is. Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... \n\nWait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Like... physically? No, I don't think they're going to come over here and, you know, attack me or anything. It's not like that. It's more like they're just waiting for me to fail. Like I told you, I know how crazy it sounds, I really do. But I'm telling you, I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Everything is ruined. And I can just tell the neighbors are watching, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nIt's like they have nothing better to do with their time. Especially since the neighborhood association stopped doing those monthly block parties. They used to have them every first Sunday of the month, right down at the end of the cul-de-sac. Someone would always bring this massive inflatable bounce house for the kids. One time, it got really windy, and the stakes came loose, and the whole thing almost blew into Mrs. Gable's prized rose bushes. She would have been furious. She spends hours out there with these tiny little pruning shears, clipping away at them. She won first prize at the county fair three years in a row for her yellow roses. My wife tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles just decimated them. We tried spraying them with this organic neem oil stuff, but it smelled terrible, like rancid garlic and peanut butter...\n\nBut, yeah, no, they don't want to hurt me physically. They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are talking about you specifically when they're outside?\n\nPatient: Talking about me? Yeah... I mean, I see them out there, leaning over the property lines. They whisper. It’s like high school all over again. My daughter hated high school for that exact reason. She went to West Monroe, and the girls there were just brutal. She had this one friend, Sarah, who spread a rumor about her stealing a graphing calculator from the math lab. Can you believe that? A TI-84. Those things were like a hundred bucks back then. I remember buying it at Circuit City before they all closed down. Circuit City had that big red plug logo... I used to love wandering the aisles there on weekends, looking at the huge stereo systems. We bought our first CD player there in the nineties, it was a Sony, held five discs at once... \n\nBut, um, what were we... right, the neighbors. Yes, they talk about me. I know I keep saying this today, and I know you probably think it's completely far-fetched, but they are definitely watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. They know about the house. They know that the mold and the bad wiring have completely poisoned everything I own. They're just standing out there whispering about it, waiting for me to finally lose it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors' conversations are specifically about you when they whisper?\n\nPatient: Yeah, when they whisper... I mean, what else would they be whispering about? It's always when I'm out there or when they think I'm looking out the window. They huddle up. Just like those guys at the hardware store used to do. I worked at a True Value for about six months back in my twenties. The manager, this guy named Phil, he was always whispering with the cashiers. Phil drove this beat-up old El Camino, faded blue with a rusted-out tailgate. He was so proud of that car, always talking about how he was going to restore it, put a new engine in it, paint it cherry red. He kept a bucket of those pine tree air fresheners in the cab, so it always smelled like cheap cologne and cedar... \n\nAnyway, I don't even know why I thought of Phil. But yes, the neighbors. They're whispering about me. Like I keep telling you—and I know it sounds completely far-fetched, I really do—but I am certain that the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. It's all ruined. And they know it. They're just watching and whispering, waiting to see when I mess up again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors' whispering is directly about you or something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's about me. I mean, they're not whispering about the weather, you know? They look right at my house. It's like when you go to a restaurant and you know the waiter is talking about you to the bartender. We used to go to this diner, Mel's, over on 5th Street. They had the best cherry pie. Not that canned stuff, but real cherries, tart and sweet. My wife loved it. We'd go every Friday night, sit in the same red vinyl booth... booth number four, right by the jukebox. That jukebox had all the classics. I remember playing 'Dock of the Bay' on it once, and the needle got stuck, just kept repeating the same whistled note over and over until the cook had to come out and smack the side of the machine. He had this big grease stain on his apron...\n\nBut, uh, anyway, I know I've said this a hundred times today, and I know how crazy it sounds. I really do. Control my thoughts? I don't know... I guess I try to, but they just sort of go where they want. It's like trying to steer a car when the alignment is shot. I had this old Ford Taurus back in the late nineties, and the steering on that thing was just awful. If you took your hands off the wheel for even a second, it would pull hard to the right. I remember driving it down I-95 in the pouring rain, the wipers were making this terrible screeching noise because the rubber was completely worn down. I meant to replace them at AutoZone, but I kept forgetting. AutoZone always smelled like fresh tires and motor oil... I used to buy my windshield wiper fluid there, the kind that melts the ice in the winter. Winter used to be so much colder when I was a kid...

But, um, what was the question? My thoughts... right. I don't know if they control me, exactly. But I can't stop thinking about the house. I know I keep bringing it up, and I know you probably think it's completely far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring have completely poisoned everything I own. I can't stop thinking about it because it's true, and I know the neighbors are just waiting for me to mess up again.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... my wife made the appointment. She's the one who's scared. We've been in that house for fifteen years, and honestly, the maintenance is just... it's a lot. I haven't been working, so I try to keep an eye on things, but the plumbing is old. We had a guy out a few years ago, charged us an arm and a leg just to look at the water heater. He drove this loud yellow van that leaked oil all over my driveway, which took me two days to scrub out. \n\nBut anyway, what finally scared her—why she made me come in—is that I keep hearing water running inside the walls. And sometimes I see this dark, wet patch spreading across the hallway floor. I go over, I touch it, I check it... and it's dry. Completely dry. I used to lay down towels, you know, the good blue ones we got for our anniversary, just in case the wood warped, but... I don't know. My brother had water damage once and it completely ruined his foundation, so you can't be too careful.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the whispering in the walls?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been going on for about seven months now. Yeah, right around the time of that awful winter freeze we had. You remember how bad the roads got? I actually had to go out in it because my wife's sedan has terrible traction. Those tires were practically bald since we bought them at that discount place off the interstate. I told her not to go there, the guys in the shop always try to upsell you on alignment packages you don't even need, but she likes a bargain. \n\nAnyway, the freeze... it just turned all the house repairs I was trying to manage into this massive mess. I just couldn't get out from under it. I had been trying to patch up the baseboards in the guest room, and then the cold hit, and the materials wouldn't set right, and I was spending hours just sitting on the floor staring at the putty... I don't know. It just felt like everything was piling up and collapsing all at once. But yeah, that's when I started hearing them. Seven months. Ever since the freeze ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the whispering in the walls is actually people talking, or could it be something else?\n\nPatient: People talking? Well... I know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. But I’m certain the mold and the wiring in that house have poisoned everything I own. Everything. \n\nThe wiring is that old copper stuff, you know? We had an electrician out back in... oh, it must have been 2008. He had this little terrier that just sat in the front seat of his truck the whole time, barking its head off while he looked at our breaker box. But he didn't even fix the moisture problem. He just charged us and left. So now the dampness and the bad wires, it's all mixed together and it's just... toxic. It seeped into my clothes, my old work boots, my papers... everything is ruined. \n\nAnd the whispering... well, I can tell the neighbors are watching to see when I mess up again. Gary across the street is always out there messing with his sprinkler heads. Nobody needs to adjust their sprinklers three times a week. He just stands there on his lawn, pretending to work on them, but he's really just staring at my windows, waiting for me to finally collapse.\n\nDoctor: Do you really think Gary is watching you, or is that just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: It's not a feeling. I know how it sounds to you, I know it sounds far-fetched, but I am absolutely certain he's watching me. They all are. They're just waiting to see when I mess up again. And they know about the house. I'm telling you, the mold and that old wiring, it has completely poisoned everything I own. Everything in there is contaminated. My clothes, my tools... \n\nI had this really good set of Craftsman wrenches I bought at Sears, back when the one at the mall was still open. You remember that mall? They tore the whole thing down a few years ago to build those luxury apartments. The ones with those weird glass balconies. I don't know who would want to sit on a glass balcony right over the interstate, the noise must be terrible. My wife's sister lives near a highway and she can't even open her windows in the summer because of the exhaust from the semi-trucks. She tried planting these tall arborvitae bushes to block the sound, but they all died because the soil was too acidic. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, the wrenches. They're ruined. Poisoned by the house. And Gary knows it. He just stands out there pretending to adjust his sprinklers, just watching my front door, waiting for me to completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can do anything to stop Gary from watching you or to prevent the house from getting worse?\n\nPatient: Stop him? No... I don't think I can. I tried pulling the blinds down, but they're those cheap plastic ones, you know? We got them at Home Depot back when they had that huge clearance sale. Actually, it was the exact same day I bought this heavy-duty bleach to try and scrub the walls. People think bleach kills mold, but it really doesn't. It just bleaches the spores white so you can't see them. My buddy Dave told me that. He used to do HVAC work before he ruined his back. He slipped on some black ice outside a gas station and broke two vertebrae. He had to wear one of those awful halo braces for months. It looked so uncomfortable, having metal literally screwed into your skull... I don't know how he slept. He said he had to sleep sitting straight up in a brown leather recliner for half a year. \n\nBut... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, the house. Right. Like I said before, I know it sounds far-fetched. I know exactly how it sounds to a doctor. But I am just... I'm certain the mold and that wiring have poisoned everything I own. I can't stop it from getting worse because it's already in everything, the damage is done. And I can't stop Gary. I can just tell he and the rest of the neighbors are watching, just waiting for me to finally mess up for good. There's just nothing I can do about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have any control over what happens to you and your house?\n\nPatient: Control? No, no control at all. I mean, how can I? Everything is already ruined. I haven't been able to keep up with any of it... the repairs, paying the bills, none of it. My wife, she’s had to take over everything. All the decisions I used to handle, she’s doing it now. She's been so stressed, I can see it in her face. She used to love gardening to relax, you know? She had these beautiful tomato plants out back, these huge Big Boys and some cherry tomatoes. We used to make our own salsa every August. We'd get the jalapeños from the farmer's market downtown... the one they hold on Saturdays next to the old courthouse. They have this guy there who sells the most incredible raw honey. He keeps his bees out by the county line, near where that old drive-in theater used to be before it burned down in the nineties. I remember going there when I was a kid to see a double feature, sitting in the back of my dad's station wagon... \n\nBut, right, the house. Yeah, I can't do anything about it. Things got so bad that my wife actually called one of those crisis teams on me. She had to. There was some kind of safety inspection they wanted to do at the house, and I just couldn't cooperate. I couldn't let them in. Because if they came in, they'd see the poison, or they'd track more of it around, and Gary would see them, and... I just couldn't do it. So, no. I don't have any control left.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors are plotting against you specifically?\n\nPatient: Plotting? I don't know if they have, like, secret meetings about it, but yes, it's me they're focused on. Like I keep saying... and I know exactly how far-fetched it sounds to you, I really do... but I am absolutely certain they are watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nGary especially. He'll stand out by his mailbox forever, just staring at my place. He gets his mail from that same guy we've had on our route for fifteen years. Carl, I think his name is. Carl always wears those blue uniform shorts, even in the dead of December. I don't understand how his legs don't freeze. I bought a pair of heavy thermal long johns once for a hunting trip, back when I used to hunt with my brother-in-law. We went up to this little cabin near the Canadian border, and it was so cold the pipes froze solid the very first night. We had to scoop snow into a pot and melt it on the woodstove just to make our morning coffee. The coffee tasted awful, like pine needles and ash, but we drank it anyway because we were just shivering so hard... \n\nWait, why was I talking about the cabin? Oh, Gary. Right. No, he's watching me. They all are. And they know my house is ruined. I know I've said it a hundred times today, but the mold and that bad wiring... it has poisoned everything I own. Everything is just contaminated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think Gary or the neighbors want to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Like... physically? No, I don't think they're going to come over here and, you know, attack me or anything. It's not like that. It's more like they're just waiting for me to fail. Like I told you, I know how crazy it sounds, I really do. But I'm telling you, I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. Everything is ruined. And I can just tell the neighbors are watching, waiting to see when I mess up again. \n\nIt's like they have nothing better to do with their time. Especially since the neighborhood association stopped doing those monthly block parties. They used to have them every first Sunday of the month, right down at the end of the cul-de-sac. Someone would always bring this massive inflatable bounce house for the kids. One time, it got really windy, and the stakes came loose, and the whole thing almost blew into Mrs. Gable's prized rose bushes. She would have been furious. She spends hours out there with these tiny little pruning shears, clipping away at them. She won first prize at the county fair three years in a row for her yellow roses. My wife tried growing roses once, but the Japanese beetles just decimated them. We tried spraying them with this organic neem oil stuff, but it smelled terrible, like rancid garlic and peanut butter...\n\nBut, yeah, no, they don't want to hurt me physically. They're just watching. Waiting for me to finally crack.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors are talking about you specifically when they're outside?\n\nPatient: Talking about me? Yeah... I mean, I see them out there, leaning over the property lines. They whisper. It’s like high school all over again. My daughter hated high school for that exact reason. She went to West Monroe, and the girls there were just brutal. She had this one friend, Sarah, who spread a rumor about her stealing a graphing calculator from the math lab. Can you believe that? A TI-84. Those things were like a hundred bucks back then. I remember buying it at Circuit City before they all closed down. Circuit City had that big red plug logo... I used to love wandering the aisles there on weekends, looking at the huge stereo systems. We bought our first CD player there in the nineties, it was a Sony, held five discs at once... \n\nBut, um, what were we... right, the neighbors. Yes, they talk about me. I know I keep saying this today, and I know you probably think it's completely far-fetched, but they are definitely watching me, just waiting to see when I mess up again. They know about the house. They know that the mold and the bad wiring have completely poisoned everything I own. They're just standing out there whispering about it, waiting for me to finally lose it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors' conversations are specifically about you when they whisper?\n\nPatient: Yeah, when they whisper... I mean, what else would they be whispering about? It's always when I'm out there or when they think I'm looking out the window. They huddle up. Just like those guys at the hardware store used to do. I worked at a True Value for about six months back in my twenties. The manager, this guy named Phil, he was always whispering with the cashiers. Phil drove this beat-up old El Camino, faded blue with a rusted-out tailgate. He was so proud of that car, always talking about how he was going to restore it, put a new engine in it, paint it cherry red. He kept a bucket of those pine tree air fresheners in the cab, so it always smelled like cheap cologne and cedar... \n\nAnyway, I don't even know why I thought of Phil. But yes, the neighbors. They're just watching and whispering, waiting to see when I mess up again.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the neighbors' whispering is directly about you or something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's about me. I mean, they're not whispering about the weather, you know? They look right at my house. It's like when you go to a restaurant and you know the waiter is talking about you to the bartender. We used to go to this diner, Mel's, over on 5th Street. They had the best cherry pie. Not that canned stuff, but real cherries, tart and sweet. My wife loved it. We'd go every Friday night, sit in the same red vinyl booth... booth number four, right by the jukebox. That jukebox had all the classics. I remember playing 'Dock of the Bay' on it once, and the needle got stuck, just kept repeating the same whistled note over and over until the cook had to come out and smack the side of the machine. He had this big grease stain on his apron...\n\nBut, uh, anyway, I know I've said this a hundred times today, and I know how crazy it sounds. I really do. But I am absolutely certain that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's all ruined. And the neighbors, they know it. They're just watching, whispering, waiting for me to mess up again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you think about, or do your thoughts feel like they're controlling you?\n\nPatient: Control my thoughts? Connected? I mean, they make sense to me, mostly. It's just... there's a lot of them. Like a string of lights where one bulb goes out, and you have to check every single one to find the bad one. We had these old Christmas lights when I was a kid, the big glass ones, C9s I think they called them. They got so hot you could actually burn your fingers on them. My dad used to string them up around the gutters every Thanksgiving weekend. He had this aluminum ladder that was always wobbly on the left side because the rubber foot fell off. He tried fixing it with duct tape, but duct tape never really holds up in the cold, does it? It just gets brittle and peels right off. I used to hate holding the bottom of that ladder, my hands would just freeze... 

But, uh, disjointed... I don't know. Maybe they are. It's hard to tell. It's all contaminated and ruined. And those neighbors, they know it. They're just watching and whispering, waiting for me to make another mistake.

I can't stop thinking about it because it's true, and I know the neighbors are just waiting for me to mess up again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are connected and make sense to you, or do they seem disjointed and hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Connected? I mean, they make sense to me, mostly. It's just... there's a lot of them. Like a string of lights where one bulb goes out, and you have to check every single one to find the bad one. We had these old Christmas lights when I was a kid, the big glass ones, C9s I think they called them. They got so hot you could actually burn your fingers on them. My dad used to string them up around the gutters every Thanksgiving weekend. He had this aluminum ladder that was always wobbly on the left side because the rubber foot fell off. He tried fixing it with duct tape, but duct tape never really holds up in the cold, does it? It just gets brittle and peels right off. I used to hate holding the bottom of that ladder, my hands would just freeze... \n\nBut, uh, disjointed... I don't know. Maybe they are. It's hard to tell. But the main thing, the thing that never leaves my head—and I know I keep saying this, I know exactly how crazy it sounds—but I am absolutely positive that the mold and the bad wiring in that house have completely poisoned everything I own. It's all contaminated and ruined. And those neighbors, they know it. They're just watching and whispering, waiting for me to make another mistake.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find it hard to stay focused on one thought?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 49 / not_in_workforce / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
